# PULITIKLAN portrait research

Reviewed 15 September 2026. **1227 of 2408 profiles have sourced portraits**, including 1075 of 1753 people in family trees. 1181 profiles remain without an integrated portrait. Coverage includes directory-only officials and historical profiles. Source formats include local files and inherited remote images.

## Findings and source standards

A photograph is matched through a named source page, caption, official biography or a Commons description corroborated by the person’s role. A familiar face or a shared surname alone is insufficient. Group-photo frames isolate only the person explicitly identified by the caption. Namesakes, cemetery images without a face, and a known deleted copyright-violation image were excluded. The historical Ismael Mathay Jr. and Anacleto Alcala Sr. images are sourced painted portraits, identified as such in their source metadata.

Commons records retain their stated author, license and source. Official government portraits retain publisher credit; where an image-specific reuse statement is absent, that uncertainty is stated rather than inventing a Creative Commons license. A government domain alone is not proof that every image is public domain. Historical photographs and upload dates do not establish a current office or the date a photograph was taken.

All included images are served from the site, with their original source linked in the profile. CSS framing changes the visible region without generating or retouching faces. Janella Estrada’s image was extracted from a named NACC publication. Some historical photographs are small or less sharp than modern portraits.

## Coverage limits

Recorded research routes vary by profile and are listed below; unresolved entries remain open for further research. Some government image endpoints returned access challenges or no longer supplied their linked files. Others offered a named photo but insufficient provenance, or only unrelated people, monuments or paintings. These are unresolved retrieval or verification findings—not claims that no photograph exists anywhere. No complete-coverage claim is made.
